\displaystyle C_{2}(\min(a_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2}),b_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2})))^{2}(\frac{c_{r}(\xi)}{2})^{3+\gamma}(2^{\frac{3+\gamma}{2}})^{n}\xi^{\frac{n}{2}}\frac{1}{2^{n+1}(\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})2^{\frac{n}{2}})}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle C_{2}(\min(a_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2}),b_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2})))^{2}(\frac{c_{r}(\xi)}{2})^{3+\gamma}(2^{\frac{3+\gamma}{2}})^{n}\xi^{\frac{n}{2}}\frac{1}{4}\frac{1}{2^{n}}\min\{\frac{1}{\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})},\frac{1}{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})2^{\frac{n}{2}}}\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle C_{2}(\min(a_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2}),b_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2})))^{2}(\frac{c_{r}(\xi)}{2})^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{4}(2^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\xi^{\frac{1}{2}})^{n}\min\{\frac{1}{\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})},\frac{1}{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})2^{\frac{n}{2}}}\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle C_{2}(\min(a_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2}),b_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2})))^{2}(\frac{c_{r}(\xi)}{2})^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{4}(2^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\xi^{\frac{1}{2}})^{n}

\displaystyle\times\min\left\{\frac{1}{\max\{\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\}},\frac{1}{\max\{\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\}2^{\frac{n}{2}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle C_{2}(\min(a_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2}),b_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2})))^{2}(\frac{c_{r}(\xi)}{2})^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{4(\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))}(2^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\xi^{\frac{1}{2}})^{n}\frac{1}{2^{\frac{n}{2}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle C_{2}(\min(a_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2}),b_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2})))^{2}\left(\frac{c_{r}(\xi)}{2}\right)^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{4(\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))}(2^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\xi^{\frac{1}{2}})^{n}.

By defining

(4.41)

C_{3}:=C_{2}(\frac{c_{r}(\xi)}{2})^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{4(\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))},\ \lambda:=\min\{1,2^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\xi^{\frac{1}{2}},C_{3}2^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\xi^{\frac{1}{2}}\},

we have

(4.42)

a_{n,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2})\geq(\min(a_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2}),b_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2})))^{2}\lambda^{n}.

Next, note that by (
4.22
) and (
4.23
), we have

b_{0,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2})\leq a_{0,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2}),\ b_{1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2})\leq a_{1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2}).

Then, we define the following numbers:

(4.43)

l_{n}:=\min\{1\leq l\leq n-1\mid a_{n-l,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2})\geq b_{n-l,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2})\}.

In doing so, we can iterate (
4.42
) and use (
4.40
) to derive:

(4.44)

\begin{split}a_{n,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2})&\geq\lambda^{n+2(n-1)+...+2^{l_{n}-1}(n-l_{n}+1)}(b_{n-l_{n},\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2}))^{2^{l_{n}}}\\
&\geq\lambda^{n+2(n-1)+...+2^{l_{n}-1}(n-l_{n}+1)}\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2^{n-l_{n}}2^{l_{n}}}\\
&\geq\lambda^{\sum_{i=0}^{l_{n}-1}2^{i}(n-i)}\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2^{n}}.\end{split}

Next, we notice that

(4.45)

\begin{split}&\sum_{i=0}^{l_{n}-1}2^{i}(n-i)\leq 2^{l_{n}}(n-l_{n}+2).\end{split}

Then, we notice that for any
0\leq m\leq n-1<\infty

(4.46)

\begin{split}&2^{n-1-m}(m+3)\leq 2^{n+1}.\end{split}

By replacing
m
in (
4.46
) by
n-l_{n}-1
, we have

2^{l_{n}}(n-l_{n}+2)\leq 2^{n+1}.

As a result, we conclude from (
4.44
), (
4.45
), and (
4.46
) that

\displaystyle a_{n,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau_{2})\geq\lambda^{2^{l_{n}}(n-l_{n}+2)}\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2^{n}}\geq\lambda^{2^{n+1}}\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2^{n}}.

Notice that we used the fact that
\lambda\leq 1
. Hence, by defining
\mathcal{A}_{2}(\tau_{2}):=\lambda^{2}\mathcal{A}_{1}(\tau_{2})
, we conclude the proof of
Theorem
1.1
in the case
0\leq\alpha<1
where

t\in\left[\tau^{\prime\prime}+\min\left\{\frac{\delta_{T,D}(\tau^{\prime\prime})-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{2},\tau^{\prime\prime}\right\},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\delta_{T,D}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right].

To deduce the
Theorem
1.2
from
Theorem
1.1
.
We observe that

We consider the first term of (
1.12
) and (
1.13
). Then we use
Lemma
3.1
to control the damping effect along the characteristic line
X_{s,t}(x,v)
. Thus, the solution is a super solution of the damp transport equation. That is, we either have

(4.47)

\begin{split}f(t,x,v)&\geq f_{0}(X_{0,t}(x,v),v)e^{-tC_{L}(1+|v|^{2})},\end{split}

when
t\leq t_{\partial}(x,v)
, or

(4.48)

\begin{split}f(t,x,v)&=\alpha f(t-t_{\partial}(x,v),X_{t-t_{\partial}(x,v),t}(x,v),R(X_{t-t_{\partial}(x,v),t}(x,v),v))\\
&\hskip 14.22636pte^{-t_{\partial}(x,v)C_{L}(1+|v|^{2})}\\
&+(1-\alpha)\left(\int_{w\cdot n(X_{t-t_{\partial}(x,v),t}(x,v))>0}f(t,X_{t-t_{\partial}(x,v),t}(x,v),w)(w\cdot n(X_{t-t_{\partial}(x,v),t}(x,v)))dw\right)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\frac{1}{2\pi T_{B}^{2}}e^{-\frac{|v|^{2}}{2T_{B}}}e^{-t_{\partial}(x,v)C_{L}(1+|v|^{2})},\end{split}

when
t\geq t_{\partial}(x,v)
.
Then
Theorem
1.2
can be deduced from the comparison principle.

5.
Maxwellian bound in the cut-off case for fully specular reflection condition

In this chapter, we consider the fully specular reflection condition
\alpha=1
.

We first introduce with omitted proof the following estimate of the lower bound of away from the boundary, which is similar to
Proposition 4.1,
[
2
]
. We note that the argument does not use the convexity of
\Omega
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Suppose that
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfies
Assumption A
and that the collision kernel
B
satisfies
Assumption B
with
\nu<0
. We consider a continuous mild solution
f(t,x,v)
of (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) with
\alpha=1
. Fix
\tau^{\prime\prime}
as in
Proposition
3.6
. Then, for any
\tau^{\prime}\in\left(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\delta_{T}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right]
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
, the following holds: for any
1\leq n\leq N
,
0<l\leq\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
, and

\mathfrak{R}>\max\left\{\frac{l}{\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime}},\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+(\frac{3\sqrt{2}}{4})^{N}\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right\},

if
(t,x,v)\in\left[\tau^{\prime\prime}+\frac{l}{2^{n}\mathfrak{R}},\tau^{\prime}\right]\times\overline{\Omega}\times B(0,\mathfrak{R})
, and there exists
t_{1}\in\left[\tau^{\prime\prime},t-\frac{l}{2^{n}\mathfrak{R}}\right]
, we have
X_{t_{1},t}(x,v)\in(\Omega-\Omega_{l})\cap B\left(x_{i},\frac{\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{n}}\right)
for some
1\leq i\leq\mathcal{N}(N,\tau^{\prime\prime})
, then we have

(5.1)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ f(t,x,v)\geq a^{S}_{n}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime},\mathfrak{R})\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{S}_{n})}(V_{t_{1},t}(x,v)).

Here, the notation
\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
,
\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
,
\delta_{T}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
, and
\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
are as in
Proposition
3.6
and may depend on
N
. The sequence
r^{S}_{n}
and
a^{S}_{n}(l,\tau^{\prime})
are defined by

(5.2)

\begin{cases}r^{S}_{0}(\tau^{\prime\prime})=\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\\
r^{S}_{n+1}(\tau^{\prime\prime})=\frac{3\sqrt{2}}{4}r^{S}_{n}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\end{cases}

(5.3)

\begin{cases}a^{S}_{0}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime},\mathfrak{R})=a_{0}(\tau^{\prime\prime})e^{-C_{L}(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left\langle\mathfrak{R}\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}},\\
a^{S}_{n+1}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime},\mathfrak{R})=C_{Q}\frac{(r^{S}_{n}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))^{3+\gamma}l}{2^{n+4}\mathfrak{R}}e^{-(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})C_{L}\left\langle\mathfrak{R}\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}a^{S}_{n}\left(\frac{l}{8},\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime},\mathfrak{R}\right)^{2},\end{cases}

where we recall that
a_{0}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
,
\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
are defined in
Proposition
3.6
.

Proof.

We prove (
5.1
) by using an induction on n. To show that (
5.1
) holds in the case
n=0
, we first consider the first term of the right hand side of (
1.14
), which implies by
Lemma
3.1
that

(5.4)

\begin{split}f(t,x,v)\geq f(t_{1},X_{t_{1},t}(x,v),V_{t_{1},t}(x,v))e^{-(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})C_{L}\left\langle\mathfrak{R}\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}.\end{split}

Then we use
Proposition
3.6
to derive

(5.5)

\begin{split}&f(t_{1},X_{t_{1},t}(x,v),V_{t_{1},t}(x,v))\\
&\geq a_{0}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\mathbf{1}_{(B(x_{i},\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))\cap\overline{\Omega})\times B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))}(X_{t_{1},t}(x,v),V_{t_{1},t}(x,v))\\
&=a_{0}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))}(V_{t_{1},t}(x,v)),\end{split}

where we used the assumption that
X_{t_{1},t}(x,v)\in(\Omega-\Omega_{l})\cap B\left(x_{i},\frac{\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{n}}\right)
.
By combining (
5.4
) and (
5.5
), we proved the case
n=0
.

Now, we assume that the case
n=k\leq N-1
holds. Given
0<l\leq\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
,
(t,x,v)\in[\tau^{\prime\prime}+\frac{l}{2^{k+1}\mathfrak{R}},\tau^{\prime}]\times\overline{\Omega}\times B(0,\mathfrak{R})
with
t_{1}\in[\tau^{\prime\prime},t-\frac{l}{2^{k+1}\mathfrak{R}}]
such that
X_{t_{1},t}(x,v)\in(\Omega-\Omega_{l})\cap B(x_{i},\frac{\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{k+1}})
, we consider the second term of the right hand side of (
1.14
) and use
Lemma
3.1
to derive that

(5.6)

\begin{split}&f(t,x,v)\\
\geq&e^{-(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})C_{L}\left\langle\mathfrak{R}\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}\\
&\int_{t_{1}+\frac{l}{2^{k+3}\mathfrak{R}}}^{t_{1}+\frac{l}{2^{k+2}\mathfrak{R}}}Q^{+}[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot),f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](V_{s,t}(x,v))\,ds,\end{split}

where we use the fact that
t\geq t_{1}+\frac{l}{2^{k+2}\mathfrak{R}}
.
Next, we notice that

(5.7)

\begin{split}|X_{t_{1},t}(x,v)-X_{s,t}(x,v)|\leq(s-t_{1})|v|<\frac{l}{2^{k+2}}\end{split}

for any
(t,x,v)\in[\tau^{\prime\prime}+\frac{l}{2^{k+1}\mathfrak{R}},\tau^{\prime}]\times\overline{\Omega}\times B(0,\mathfrak{R})
with
t_{1}\in[\tau^{\prime\prime},t-\frac{l}{2^{k+1}\mathfrak{R}}]
,
s\in[t_{1}+\frac{l}{2^{k+3}\mathfrak{R}},t_{1}+\frac{l}{2^{k+2}\mathfrak{R}}]
, which implies that

(5.8)

X_{s,t}(x,v)\in\Omega-\Omega_{l-\frac{l}{2^{k+2}}}.

We also deduce that

(5.9)

V_{s,t}(x,v)=V_{t_{1},t}(x,v)

since under specular reflection, the backward characteristic velocity is piecewise constant and identical on
[t_{1},s]
without collisions.
Next, we notice that for
u\in B(0,\mathfrak{R})
,

(5.10)

\begin{split}&|X_{t_{1},s}(X_{s,t}(x,v),u)-X_{t_{1},t}(x,v)|\\
\leq&|X_{t_{1},s}(X_{s,t}(x,v),u)-X_{s,t}(x,v)|+|X_{s,t}(x,v)-X_{t_{1},t}(x,v)|\\
\leq&(s-t_{1})|u|+(s-t_{1})|v|\leq\frac{l}{2^{k+1}}\leq\frac{\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{k+1}},\end{split}

which implies

(5.11)

X_{t_{1},s}(X_{s,t}(x,v),u)\in(\Omega-\Omega_{\frac{l}{8}})\cap B\left(x_{i},\frac{\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{k}}\right),

and

(5.12)

V_{t_{1},s}(X_{s,t}(x,v),u)=u,

for
u\in B(0,\mathfrak{R})
. In addition, for any
s\in[t_{1}+\frac{l}{2^{k+3}\mathfrak{R}},t_{1}+\frac{l}{2^{k+2}\mathfrak{R}}]
, we have

(5.13)

\tau^{\prime\prime}+\frac{\frac{l}{8}}{2^{k}\mathfrak{R}}\leq s\leq\tau^{\prime},

(5.14)

t_{1}\in\left[\tau^{\prime\prime},s-\frac{\frac{l}{8}}{2^{k}\mathfrak{R}}\right].

As a result, the assumptions of induction are satisfied and we deduce that

(5.15)

\begin{split}f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),u)\geq a^{S}_{k}\left(\frac{l}{8},\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime},\mathfrak{R}\right)\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{S}_{k})}(u).\end{split}

for
(t,x,v)\in\left[\tau^{\prime\prime}+\frac{l}{2^{k+1}\mathfrak{R}},\tau^{\prime}\right]\times\overline{\Omega}\times B(0,\mathfrak{R})
and
t_{1}\in[\tau^{\prime\prime},t-\frac{l}{2^{k+1}\mathfrak{R}}]
with
s\in\left[t_{1}+\frac{l}{2^{k+3}\mathfrak{R}},t_{1}+\frac{l}{2^{k+2}\mathfrak{R}}\right]
,
u\in B(0,\mathfrak{R})
. Here, we used (
5.12
).

Hence, we have

(5.16)

\begin{split}&Q^{+}[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot),f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](V_{s,t}(x,v))\\
\geq&a^{S}_{k}\left(\frac{l}{8},\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime},\mathfrak{R}\right)^{2}Q^{+}[\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{S}_{k})\cap B(0,\mathfrak{R})}(\cdot),\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{S}_{k})\cap B(0,\mathfrak{R})}(\cdot)](V_{s,t}(x,v))\\
=&a^{S}_{k}\left(\frac{l}{8},\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime},\mathfrak{R}\right)^{2}Q^{+}[\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{S}_{k})}(\cdot),\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{S}_{k})}(\cdot)](V_{s,t}(x,v)).\end{split}

Here, we use the fact that
\mathfrak{R}>\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+(\frac{3\sqrt{2}}{4})^{N}\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\geq|\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime})|+r^{S}_{k}
for the derivation of the last line.
Now, we use
Lemma
3.2
with
\xi=\frac{1}{2}
to derive

(5.17)

\begin{split}Q^{+}[\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{S}_{k})}(\cdot),\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{S}_{k})}(\cdot)](V_{s,t}(x,v))\geq C_{Q}(r^{S}_{k})^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{S}_{k+1})}(V_{s,t}(x,v)).\end{split}

Hence, we deduce by (
5.6
), (
5.9
), (
5.16
), and (
5.17
) that

(5.18)

\begin{split}&f(t,x,v)\\
\geq&e^{-(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})C_{L}\left\langle\mathfrak{R}\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}\int_{t_{1}+\frac{l}{2^{k+3}\mathfrak{R}}}^{t_{1}+\frac{l}{2^{k+2}\mathfrak{R}}}Q^{+}[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot),f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](V_{s,t}(x,v))\,ds\\
\geq&e^{-(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})C_{L}\left\langle\mathfrak{R}\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}\int_{t_{1}+\frac{l}{2^{k+3}\mathfrak{R}}}^{t_{1}+\frac{l}{2^{k+2}\mathfrak{R}}}a^{S}_{k}\left(\frac{l}{8},\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime},\mathfrak{R}\right)^{2}C_{Q}(r^{S}_{k})^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{S}_{k+1})}(V_{s,t}(x,v))\,ds\\
=&e^{-(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})C_{L}\left\langle\mathfrak{R}\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}a^{S}_{k}\left(\frac{l}{8},\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime},\mathfrak{R}\right)^{2}C_{Q}(r^{S}_{k})^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{2}\int_{t_{1}+\frac{l}{2^{k+3}\mathfrak{R}}}^{t_{1}+\frac{l}{2^{k+2}\mathfrak{R}}}\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{S}_{k+1})}(V_{s,t}(x,v))\,ds\\
\geq&e^{-(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})C_{L}\left\langle\mathfrak{R}\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}a^{S}_{k}\left(\frac{l}{8},\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime},\mathfrak{R}\right)^{2}C_{Q}(r^{S}_{k})^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{2}\frac{l}{2^{k+3}\mathfrak{R}}\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{S}_{k+1})}(V_{t_{1},t}(x,v))\\
\geq&a^{S}_{k+1}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime},\mathfrak{R})\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{S}_{k+1})}(V_{t_{1},t}(x,v)).\end{split}

As a result, the induction hypothesis for
n=k+1
holds. By induction, we deduce that (
5.1
) holds for
0\leq n\leq N
and we conclude the result.

∎

The following corollary is obtained by a slight modification of the previous proposition (
Corollary 4.2,
[
2
]
):

Corollary 5.2
.

Suppose that
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfies
Assumption A
and that the collision kernel
B
satisfies
Assumption B
with
\nu<0
. Let
f(t,x,v)
be a continuous mild solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) with
\alpha=1
. Fix
\tau^{\prime\prime}>0
as in
Proposition
3.6
. Then, given
\Delta^{I}_{T}\in(0,\delta_{T}(\tau^{\prime\prime})]
with
\tau^{A}\in(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\Delta^{I}_{T}]
, for all
0<l\leq\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
, there exist
a^{S}(l,\tau^{A},\tau^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{I}_{T})>0
and
\widetilde{t}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A},\Delta^{I}_{T})\in\left(0,\frac{\tau^{A}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{3}\right]
, depending on
\tau^{\prime\prime}
,
\Omega
,
M
, and
E_{f}
(and
L_{f,p}
if
\gamma<0
) such that we have the following: for
(t,x,v)\in[\tau^{A},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\Delta^{I}_{T}]\times\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, if for some
t_{1}\in[\tau^{\prime\prime},t-\widetilde{t}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A},\Delta^{I}_{T})]
we have
X_{t_{1},t}(x,v)\in\Omega-\Omega_{l}
, then we have

(5.19)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ f(t,x,v)\geq a^{S}(l,\tau^{A},\tau^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{I}_{T})\mathbf{1}_{B(0,3\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))}(v).

Here,
\mathfrak{R}_{\min},\delta_{T}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
, and
\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
are given in
Proposition
3.6
.

Proof.

Given
0<l\leq\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
and
\tau^{A}\in(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\Delta^{I}_{T}]
. We define

(5.20)

\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A}):=\left\lceil\max\left\{\log_{\frac{3\sqrt{2}}{4}}\left(\frac{4\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}\right),\log_{2}\frac{3l}{\tau^{A}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}\right\}\right\rceil,

(5.21)

\widetilde{t}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A},\Delta^{I}_{T}):=\frac{l}{2^{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}\left[\max\left\{\frac{l}{\Delta^{I}_{T}},\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+\left(\frac{3\sqrt{2}}{4}\right)^{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right\}+1\right]},

(5.22)

\mathfrak{R}_{N}^{S}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A}):=1+\max\left\{\frac{l}{\tau^{A}-\tau^{\prime\prime}},\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+\left(\frac{3\sqrt{2}}{4}\right)^{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right\}.

By
Proposition
3.6
, we know that
\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\geq 2
and
\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\leq\frac{1}{56}
, which imply
\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})\geq 8
. We observe that

(5.23)

\begin{split}\widetilde{t}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A},\Delta^{I}_{T})\leq\frac{l}{2^{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}}\leq\frac{\tau^{A}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{3}.\end{split}

The last inequality follows directly from the definition of
\widetilde{N}_{l}
, which leads to
2^{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}\geq\frac{3l}{\tau^{A}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}
.

Now, given
(t,x,v)\in[\tau^{A},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\Delta^{I}_{T}]\times\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
with
t_{1}\in[\tau^{\prime\prime},t-\widetilde{t}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A},\Delta^{I}_{T})]
such that
X_{t_{1},t}(x,v)\in\Omega-\Omega_{l}
. We consider
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\mathcal{N}(\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A}),\tau^{\prime\prime})}
from
Proposition
3.6
such that

\overline{\Omega}\subset\bigcup_{1\leq i\leq\mathcal{N}(\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A}),\tau^{\prime\prime})}B\left(x_{i},\frac{\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}}\right)

. There exists
1\leq i\leq\mathcal{N}(\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A}),\tau^{\prime\prime})
such that
X_{t_{1},t}(x,v)\in B\left(x_{i},\frac{\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}}\right)
.
Next, we find that

(5.24)

\widetilde{t}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A},\Delta^{I}_{T})\geq\frac{l}{2^{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}\mathfrak{R}_{N}^{S}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})},

which leads to

(5.25)

t_{1}\in\left[\tau^{\prime\prime},t-\frac{l}{2^{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}\mathfrak{R}_{N}^{S}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}\right].

Moreover, we have

(5.26)

\mathfrak{R}_{N}^{S}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})>\max\left\{\frac{l}{\Delta^{I}_{T}},\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+(\frac{3\sqrt{2}}{4})^{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right\}.

Thus, we apply
Proposition
5.1
with
\tau^{\prime}=\tau^{\prime\prime}+\Delta^{I}_{T}
,
n=N=\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})
, and
\mathfrak{R}=\mathfrak{R}_{N}^{S}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})
and deduce that for any

(t,x,v)\in\left[\tau^{\prime\prime}+\frac{l}{2^{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}\mathfrak{R}_{N}^{S}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\Delta^{I}_{T}\right]\times\overline{\Omega}\times B(0,\mathfrak{R}_{N}^{S}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})),

we have

\displaystyle f(t,x,v)

\displaystyle\geq a^{S}_{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\Delta^{I}_{T},\mathfrak{R}_{N}^{S}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A}))\mathbf{1}_{B\left(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{S}_{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)}(V_{t_{1},t}(x,v))

\displaystyle\geq a^{S}_{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\Delta^{I}_{T},\mathfrak{R}_{N}^{S}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A}))\mathbf{1}_{B\left(0,3\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)}(V_{t_{1},t}(x,v))

\displaystyle=a^{S}_{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\Delta^{I}_{T},\mathfrak{R}_{N}^{S}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A}))\mathbf{1}_{B(0,3\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))}(v).

Here, we used the fact that
r^{S}_{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\geq 4\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\geq 3\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+|\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime})|
for the second line.

Moreover, we have

[\tau^{A},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\Delta^{I}_{T}]\subset\left[\tau^{\prime\prime}+\frac{l}{2^{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}\mathfrak{R}_{N}^{S}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\Delta^{I}_{T}\right].

Finally, by the choice of
\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})
, we have

\mathfrak{R}_{N}^{S}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})\geq 4\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime}).

As a result, by defining

(5.27)

a^{S}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A},\Delta^{I}_{T}):=a^{S}_{\widetilde{N}_{l}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\Delta^{I}_{T},\mathfrak{R}_{N}^{S}(l,\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{A})).

we obtain the desired lower bound (
5.19
), which completes the proof.

∎

Before stating the next proposition, we recall the quantities
\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
,
\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
,
\delta_{T}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
, and
\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
from
Proposition
3.6
, and notice that
\delta_{T}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\leq\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})=\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\leq\frac{d}{56}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\geq 2
. We now introduce the following auxiliary sequences:

(5.28)

\begin{cases}r^{B}_{0}(\tau^{\prime\prime})=\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\\
r^{B}_{n+1}(\tau^{\prime\prime})=\frac{3\sqrt{2}}{4}r^{B}_{n}(\tau^{\prime\prime})-\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}.\end{cases}

Observe that
\{r^{B}_{n}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
is strictly increasing and unbounded, that

r^{B}_{0}(\tau^{\prime\prime})<1\leq\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),

and that

(5.29)

\frac{2\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+1}\geq\frac{4}{3}>\frac{3\sqrt{2}}{4}.

Hence, the following notation

(5.30)

N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}):=\min\{n\mid 2\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\geq r^{B}_{n}(\tau^{\prime\prime})>\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+1\}

is well-defined.
We also define

(5.31)

\vartheta_{n}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}):=\frac{1}{2^{n+1}\left(r^{B}_{n}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)}.

(5.32)

\delta_{T}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}):=\min\left\{t_{\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}}\left(\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+r^{B}_{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}\right),\delta_{T}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\frac{d}{4\left(\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+r_{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)}\right\},

(5.33)

a^{B}_{n,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B}):=\begin{cases}a_{0}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\ &n=0\\
\left(\min\{a^{B}_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B}),b^{B}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B})\}\right)^{2}C_{Q}(r^{B}_{n-1}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))^{3+\gamma}\\
\frac{\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{2^{n+2}(\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+r^{B}_{n}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))}e^{-C_{L}\frac{(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left\langle\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+r^{B}_{n}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}{2^{n}\left(\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+r^{B}_{n}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)}},\ &n\geq 1,\end{cases}

(5.34)

b^{B}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B}):=a^{S}\left(l_{\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}}\left(\frac{\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{6}\right),\tau^{B},\tau^{\prime\prime},\delta_{T}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right).

Here, the notation
a_{0}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
is from
Proposition
3.6
and the notation
a^{S}
is from
Corollary
5.2
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Suppose that
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfies
Assumption A
and that the collision kernel
B
satisfies
Assumption B
. Fix
d
with
0<d<\min\{1,\delta\}
as in
Lemma
2.1
. Let
f(t,x,v)
be a continuous mild solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) with
\alpha=1
and let
\tau^{\prime\prime}>0
be as given in
Proposition
3.6
. The following holds: For any
\tau^{B}\in(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\delta_{T}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})]
, there exist
a^{B}_{0}(\tau^{B})>0
, which depends on
\tau^{\prime\prime}
,
\Omega
,
M
, and
E_{f}
, such that

(5.35)

f(t,x,v)\geq\min\left\{a^{B}_{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B}),b^{B}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B})\right\}\mathbf{1}_{B(0,1)}(v)

for any

(t,x,v)\in\left[\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-\vartheta_{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right),\tau^{B}\right]\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}.

Here,
N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
is defined in (
5.30
) and
\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
is introduced in
Proposition
3.6
and
\vartheta_{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}
is from (
5.31
).

Proof.

We start in a similar way to the proof of
Proposition
4.2
. We apply
Proposition
3.6
to
\overline{\Omega}
to deduce the existence of points
\{x_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\mathcal{N}(N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\tau^{\prime\prime})}\subset\Omega
such that
\overline{\Omega}\subset\underset{1\leq j\leq\mathcal{N}(N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\tau^{\prime\prime})}{\bigcup}B(x_{j},\frac{\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}})
and

(5.36)

f(t,x,v)\geq a_{0}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{j}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))}(v)

for any
1\leq j\leq\mathcal{N}(N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\tau^{\prime\prime})
and any
(t,x,v)\in[\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\delta_{T}(\tau^{\prime\prime})]\times[B(x_{j},\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))\cap\overline{\Omega}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Then, we recall the notation
t_{\epsilon}(v_{M})
in (
2.23
) and
\delta_{T}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
in (
5.32
)

(5.37)

\delta_{T}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}):=\min\left\{t_{\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}}\left(\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+r^{B}_{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}\right),\delta_{T}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\frac{d}{4\left(\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+r_{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)}\right\},

Now, given
\tau^{B}\in(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\delta_{T}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})]
and
0\leq n\leq N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
, we show that

(5.38)

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \ f(t,x,v)\geq\min\{a^{B}_{n,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B}),b^{B}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B})\}\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{B}_{n}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))}(v),\\
&\forall(t,x)\in\left[\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-\vartheta_{n}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right),\tau^{B}\right]\times B\left(x_{i},\frac{\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{n}}\right)\end{split}

for
1\leq i\leq\mathcal{N}(N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\tau^{\prime\prime})
.

The proof is similar to the proof of
Proposition
4.2
and we use an induction on
n
. The base case
n=0
follows directly from (
5.36
). Assume that the case
n=k
holds. Given

(5.39)

\begin{split}(t,x,v)\in&\left[\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-\vartheta_{k+1}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right),\tau^{B}\right]\\
&\times B\left(x_{i},\frac{\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{k+1}}\right)\times B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{B}_{k+1}(\tau^{\prime\prime})),\end{split}

we use the Duhamel formula (
1.12
) and apply
Lemma
3.1
to deduce that

(5.40)

\begin{split}&f(t,x,v)\\
\geq&\int_{\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-2\vartheta_{k+1}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)}^{\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-\vartheta_{k+1}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)}e^{-C_{L}(t-s)\left\langle r^{B}_{k+1}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}\\
&Q^{+}[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot),f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](V_{s,t}(x,v))\,ds.\end{split}

Since for any
s\in\left[\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-2\vartheta_{k+1}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right),t\right]
, we have

(5.41)

\begin{split}&|X_{s,t}(x,v)-x_{i}|\leq|X_{s,t}(x,v)-x|+|x-x_{i}|\leq|v|(t-s)+\frac{\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{k+1}}\\
\leq&\frac{\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{2^{k+2}}+\frac{\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{k+1}}\leq\frac{\delta_{T}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{k+2}}+\frac{\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{k+1}}\leq\frac{\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{k}}<\frac{d}{8}.\end{split}

Here recall the definition of
\delta_{T}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
and
\delta_{T}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
from (
5.32
) and (
3.61
).
We next apply the induction assumption and
Lemma
3.2
to derive that

(5.42)

\begin{split}&Q^{+}[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot),f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](V_{s,t}(x,v))\\
\geq&C_{Q}(r^{B}_{k}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{2}\min\left(a^{B}_{k,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B}),b^{B}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B})\right)^{2}\mathbf{1}_{B\left(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{B}_{k+1}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}\right)}(V_{s,t}(x,v)).\end{split}

We will generate the lower bound again depending on whether the characteristic line
\{X_{s}(x,v)\}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-2\vartheta_{k+1}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)\leq s\leq t}
touches the boundary or not.

If
|V_{s,t}(x,v)-v|<\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}
for
\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-2\vartheta_{k+1}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)\leq s\leq t
, we have

(5.43)

\mathbf{1}_{B\left(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r^{B}_{k+1}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}\right)}(V_{s,t}(x,v))=1,

for
\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-2\vartheta_{k+1}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)\leq s\leq t
.
We deduce that

(5.44)

\begin{split}&f(t,x,v)\\
\geq&C_{Q}(r^{B}_{k}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{2}\min\left(a^{B}_{k,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B}),b^{B}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B})\right)^{2}\\
&\int_{\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-2\vartheta_{k+1}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)}^{\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-\vartheta_{k+1}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)}e^{-C_{L}(t-s)\langle r^{B}_{k+1}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}\,ds\\
\geq&C_{Q}(r^{B}_{k}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{2}\min\left(a^{B}_{k,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B}),b^{B}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B})\right)^{2}\frac{\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{2^{k+2}(\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+r^{B}_{k+1}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))}\\
&\times e^{-C_{L}\frac{(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left\langle\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+r^{B}_{k+1}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}{2^{k+1}\left(\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+r^{B}_{k+1}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)}}\\
=&a^{B}_{k+1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B}).\end{split}

On the other hand, if

|V_{s,t}(x,v)-v|\geq\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}
for some
\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-2\vartheta_{k+1}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)\leq s\leq t
, we have
X_{s,t}(x,v)\in\partial\Omega
for some

s\in\left[\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-2\vartheta_{k+1}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right),t\right].

By (
5.41
), it follows that
x_{i}\in\Omega_{\frac{d}{8}}
, so by
Remark
2.2
, there exists
1\leq k(i)\leq m_{1}
such that
x_{i}\in B(x^{0}_{k(i)},\frac{d}{4})
.
Notice that
|V_{s,t}(x,v)-v|\geq\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}
for some
\tau^{\prime\prime}\leq s\leq t

Using
Lemma
2.6
, we deduce that for any
0\leq\tau_{2}\leq t-\tau^{\prime\prime}
, we have

X_{s^{\prime},t}(x,v)\notin\Omega_{l_{\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}}(\tau_{2})}\cap B(x_{i}^{0},d)

for some

s^{\prime}\in[\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\tau_{2}],

which implies since
X_{s^{\prime},t}(x,v)\in B(x^{0}_{i},d)
:

X_{s^{\prime},t}(x,v)\in\Omega-\Omega_{l_{\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}}(\tau_{2})}.

Since we have

(5.45)

\begin{split}t-\tau^{\prime\prime}\geq\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-\vartheta_{k+1}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)-\tau^{\prime\prime}=\frac{\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{2},\end{split}

we can take
\tau_{2}=\frac{\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{6}
.
Next, we observe that

\begin{split}\tau^{\prime\prime}+\tau_{2}=\tau^{\prime\prime}+\frac{\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{3}\leq t-\frac{\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{3}\leq t-\widetilde{t}\left(l_{\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}}\left(\frac{\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{6}\right),\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{B},\delta_{T}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right).\end{split}

Consequently, we have

s^{\prime}\in\left[\tau^{\prime\prime},t-\widetilde{t}\left(l_{\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}}\left(\frac{\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{6}\right),\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{B},\delta_{T}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)\right]

and

X_{s^{\prime},t}(x,v)\in\Omega-\Omega_{l_{\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}}(\tau_{2})}.

We also notice that

l_{\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}}\left(\frac{\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{6}\right)=\frac{(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})}{15360}\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\leq\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime}).

Therefore, we apply
Corollary
5.2
with parameters

(l,\tau^{A},\tau^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{I}_{T})\rightarrow\left(l_{\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}}\left(\frac{\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{6}\right),\tau^{B},\tau^{\prime\prime},\delta_{T}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)

to deduce that

\displaystyle f(t,x,v)\geq a^{S}\left(l_{\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}}\left(\frac{\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{6}\right),\tau^{B},\tau^{\prime\prime},\delta_{T}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)\mathbf{1}_{B(0,3\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))}(v)

\displaystyle=a^{S}\left(l_{\frac{\delta_{V}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{40}}\left(\frac{\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime}}{6}\right),\tau^{B},\tau^{\prime\prime},\delta_{T}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right),

which coincides with the definition of
b^{B}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B})
given above.
Here, we used the fact

|v|\leq|\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime})|+r^{B}_{k+1}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\leq\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})+2\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\leq 3\mathfrak{R}_{\min}(\tau^{\prime\prime}).

Combining both cases, we conclude that the induction step holds, and hence (
5.38
) is valid for
1\leq n\leq N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
.
Finally, we set
n=N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})
in (
5.38
), and use the fact that

B(0,1)\subset B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r_{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))
(because of (
5.30
)) to conclude that for
1\ \leq i\leq\mathcal{N}(N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\tau^{\prime\prime})

(5.46)

\begin{split}f(t,x,v)&\geq\min\left\{a^{B}_{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B}),b^{B}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B})\right\}\mathbf{1}_{B(\overline{v}_{i}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),r_{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}))}(v),\\
&\geq\min\left\{a^{B}_{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B}),b^{B}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{B})\right\}\mathbf{1}_{B(0,1)}(v),\\
&\forall(t,x)\in\left[\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{B}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-\vartheta_{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right),\tau^{B}\right]\times B\left(x_{i},\frac{\delta_{X}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}{2^{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}}\right),\end{split}

which implies (
5.35
).
This completes the proof.

∎

Now, we introduce new notations:

Definition 5.1
.

Given
0<\tau^{\prime\prime}<\tau^{S}
as in
Proposition
5.3
, we define

(5.47)

\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}):=\tau^{\prime\prime}+(\tau^{S}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\left(1-\vartheta_{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right).

Clearly, we see that
\tau^{\prime\prime}<\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S})<\tau^{S}
.

Definition 5.2
.

Given
0<\tau^{\prime\prime}<\tau^{S}
, and a sequence
\{\xi_{n}\}\in(0,1)^{\mathbb{N}}
, we define the following numbers:

(5.48)

r^{\prime\prime}_{n,S}:=\begin{cases}1,\ n=0\\
\sqrt{2}(1-\xi_{n})r^{\prime\prime}_{n-1,S},\ n\geq 1,\end{cases}

and

(5.49)

a^{S}_{n,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{S}):=\begin{cases}\min\left\{a^{B}_{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{S}),b^{B}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{S})\right\},\ &n=0\\
(a^{S}_{n-1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{S}))^{2}C_{Q}(r^{\prime\prime}_{n-1,S})^{3+\gamma}\xi_{n}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S})}{2^{n+1}r^{\prime\prime}_{n,S}}\\
\times e^{-C_{L}\frac{(\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}))\left\langle r^{\prime\prime}_{n,S}\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}{2^{n}r^{\prime\prime}_{n,S}}}\ &n\geq 1,\end{cases}

where
a^{B}_{0}(\tau^{B})
is defined in
Proposition
5.3
.

Now, we present a proposition similar to
Proposition
4.2
:

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfying
Assumption A
, kernel
B
satisfying
Assumption B
, and let
f(t,x,v)
be the continuous mild solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) with
\alpha=1
,
\tau^{\prime\prime}>0
given from
Proposition
3.6
. Then, given
\tau^{S}\in(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime\prime}+\delta_{T}^{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime})]
, we have

(5.50)

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \ \ f(t,x,v)\geq a^{S}_{n,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{S})\mathbf{1}_{B(0,r^{\prime\prime}_{n,S})}(v),\\
&\forall(t,x)\in\left[\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S})+(\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}))\left(1-\frac{1}{2^{n+1}(r^{\prime\prime}_{n,S})}\right),\tau^{S}\right]\times\Omega,\end{split}

for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as the one of
Proposition
4.2
. We prove by induction on
n
. The base case holds by applying
Proposition
5.3
with the choice
\tau^{B}:=\tau^{S}
: we have

(5.51)

f(t,x,v)\geq\min\left\{a^{B}_{N_{B}(\tau^{\prime\prime}),\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{S}),b^{B}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{S})\right\}\mathbf{1}_{B(0,1)}(v)

for any

(t,x,v)\in\left[\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}),\tau^{S}\right]\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}.

We now assume that the case
n=k
holds. We consider

(5.52)

\begin{split}&(t,x,v)\in\left[\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S})+(\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}))\left(1-\frac{1}{2^{k+2}(r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S})}\right),\tau^{S}\right]\\
&\times\Omega\times B(0,r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S}).\end{split}

We recall that by the Duhamel formula (
1.14
), and apply
Lemma
3.1
to deduce that

(5.53)

\begin{split}&f(t,x,v)\\
\geq&\int_{\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S})+(\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}))(1-\frac{1}{2^{k+1}r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S}})}^{\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S})+(\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}))(1-\frac{1}{2^{k+2}r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S}})}e^{-C_{L}(t-s)\left\langle r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S}\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}\\
&Q^{+}[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot),f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](V_{s,t}(x,v))\,ds.\end{split}

Since we have

\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S})+(\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}))\left(1-\frac{1}{2^{k+1}(r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S})}\right)\geq\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S})+(\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}))\left(1-\frac{1}{2^{k+1}(r^{\prime\prime}_{k,S})}\right),

we can apply the induction assumption and deduce that

(5.54)

f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),w)\geq a^{S}_{k,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{S})\mathbf{1}_{B(0,r^{\prime\prime}_{k,S})}(w),

for any
w\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

s\in\left[\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S})+(\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}))\left(1-\frac{1}{2^{k+1}r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S}}\right),\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S})+(\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}))\left(1-\frac{1}{2^{k+1}r^{\prime\prime}_{k+2,S}}\right)\right]

, and
(t,x,v)
satisfying (
5.52
).
Thus, we deduce from (
5.54
) that

(5.55)

\begin{split}&Q^{+}[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot),f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)]\\
\geq&(a^{S}_{k,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{S}))^{2}Q^{+}[\mathbf{1}_{B(0,r^{\prime\prime}_{k,S})},\mathbf{1}_{B(0,r^{\prime\prime}_{k,S})}].\end{split}

Then, we use
Lemma
3.2
with
\xi=\xi_{k+1}
to get

(5.56)

\begin{split}&Q^{+}[\mathbf{1}_{B(0,r^{\prime\prime}_{k,S})},\mathbf{1}_{B(0,r^{\prime\prime}_{k,S})}]\\
\geq&C_{Q}(r^{\prime\prime}_{k,S})^{3+\gamma}\xi_{k+1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{B(0,r^{\prime\prime}_{k,S}\sqrt{2}(1-\xi_{k+1}))}=C_{Q}(r^{\prime\prime}_{k,S})^{3+\gamma}\xi_{k+1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{B(0,r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S})}.\end{split}

As a result, we deduce from (
5.53
), (
5.55
), and (
5.56
) the following estimate: for any
(t,x,v)
satisfying (
5.52
), we have

(5.57)

\begin{split}&f(t,x,v)\\
\geq&\int_{\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S})+(\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}))(1-\frac{1}{2^{k+1}r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S}})}^{\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S})+(\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}))(1-\frac{1}{2^{k+2}r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S}})}(a^{S}_{k,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{S}))^{2}\\
&\times e^{-C_{L}(t-s)\langle r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S}\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}C_{Q}(r^{\prime\prime}_{k,S})^{3+\gamma}\xi_{k+1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{B(0,r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S})}(V_{s,t}(x,v))\,ds\\
\geq&\int_{\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S})+(\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}))(1-\frac{1}{2^{k+1}r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S}})}^{\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S})+(\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}))(1-\frac{1}{2^{k+2}r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S}})}(a^{S}_{k,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{S}))^{2}\\
&\times e^{-C_{L}(t-s)\langle r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S}\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}C_{Q}(r^{\prime\prime}_{k,S})^{3+\gamma}\xi_{k+1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\,ds\\
\geq&(a^{S}_{k,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{S}))^{2}C_{Q}(r^{\prime\prime}_{k,S})^{3+\gamma}\xi_{k+1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S})}{2^{k+2}r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S}}\\
&\times e^{-C_{L}\frac{(\tau^{S}-\widetilde{\tau}(\tau^{\prime\prime},\tau^{S}))\left\langle r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S}\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}}{2^{k+1}r^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,S}}}\\
=&a^{S}_{k+1,\tau^{\prime\prime}}(\tau^{S}).\end{split}

Hence, we showed that (
5.50
) holds for
n=k+1
and conclude the proof by induction.

∎

Thanks to
Proposition
5.4
, we can use the exact same method used at the end of the fourth chapter to derive the Maxwellian lower bound for the fully specular reflection boundary condition.

6.
Lower bound for the non-cutoff case

In this chapter, we investigate lower bounds for the solution of the Boltzmann equation in the non-cutoff case, that is,
\nu\geq 0
. Here, the domain
\Omega
is not necessarily convex.
In contrast to the cutoff case studied in Chapter 2–4, the non-cutoff setting requires a refined decomposition of the collision operator to handle the angular singularity:

(6.1)

\begin{split}Q[h_{1},h_{2}](v)&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(|v-v_{*}|,\cos{\theta})[h_{2}(v^{\prime})h_{1}(v^{\prime}_{*})-h_{2}(v)h_{1}(v_{*})]\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(|v-v_{*}|,\cos{\theta})[h_{1}(v^{\prime}_{*})(h_{2}(v^{\prime})-h_{2}(v))]\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt-h_{2}(v)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(|v-v_{*}|,\cos{\theta})(h_{1}(v_{*})-h_{1}(v^{\prime}_{*}))\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma\\
&=:Q^{1}_{b}[h_{1},h_{2}](v)-Q^{2}_{b}[h_{1},h_{2}](v),\end{split}

where

\displaystyle Q^{1}_{b}[h_{1},h_{2}](v):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(|v-v_{*}|,\cos{\theta})[h_{1}(v^{\prime}_{*})(h_{2}(v^{\prime})-h_{2}(v))]\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma,

\displaystyle Q^{2}_{b}[h_{1},h_{2}](v):=h_{2}(v)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(|v-v_{*}|,\cos{\theta})[h_{1}(v_{*})-h_{1}(v^{\prime}_{*})]\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma.

With this notation and (for some
\epsilon>0
) the decomposition of
B(|v-v_{*}|,\cos{\theta})

(6.2)

\displaystyle B(|v-v_{*}|,\cos{\theta})=\Phi(|v-v_{*}|)b(\cos{\theta})\mathbf{1}_{\theta\geq\epsilon}+\Phi(|v-v_{*}|)b(\cos{\theta})\mathbf{1}_{\theta<\epsilon},

we can consider the singular and non-singular parts of
Q
:

\displaystyle Q[h_{1},h_{2}](v)=Q^{+}_{\epsilon}[h_{1},h_{2}](v)-Q^{-}_{\epsilon}[h_{1},h_{2}](v)+Q^{1}_{\epsilon}[h_{1},h_{2}](v)+Q^{2}_{\epsilon}[h_{1},h_{2}](v),

where

\displaystyle Q^{+}_{\epsilon}[h_{1},h_{2}](v):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Phi(|v-v_{*}|)b(\cos{\theta})\mathbf{1}_{\theta\geq\epsilon}[h_{2}(v^{\prime})h_{1}(v^{\prime}_{*})]\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma,

\displaystyle Q^{-}_{\epsilon}[h_{1},h_{2}](v):=h_{2}(v)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Phi(|v-v_{*}|)b(\cos{\theta})\mathbf{1}_{\theta\geq\epsilon}h_{1}(v_{*})\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma,

\displaystyle Q^{1}_{\epsilon}[h_{1},h_{2}](v):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Phi(|v-v_{*}|)b(\cos{\theta})\mathbf{1}_{\theta<\epsilon}\left[h_{1}(v^{\prime}_{*})(h_{2}(v^{\prime})-h_{2}(v))\right]\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma,

\displaystyle Q^{2}_{\epsilon}[h_{1},h_{2}](v):=h_{2}(v)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Phi(|v-v_{*}|)b(\cos{\theta})\mathbf{1}_{\theta<\epsilon}[h_{1}(v_{*})-h_{1}(v^{\prime}_{*})]\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma.

We further introduce the following notations

(6.3)

\displaystyle b^{CO}_{\epsilon}(\cos{\theta}):=\mathbf{1}_{\theta\geq\epsilon}b(\cos{\theta}),\,b^{NCO}_{\epsilon}(\cos{\theta}):=\mathbf{1}_{\theta<\epsilon}b(\cos{\theta}),

(6.4)

\displaystyle m_{b}:=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos{\theta})(1-\cos{\theta})\,d\sigma,

(6.5)

\displaystyle n_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon}}:=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b^{CO}_{\epsilon}(\cos{\theta})\,d\sigma,\,m_{b^{NCO}_{\epsilon}}:=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b^{NCO}_{\epsilon}(\cos{\theta})(1-\cos{\theta})\,d\sigma,

\displaystyle L_{\epsilon}(h)(v):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Phi(|v-v_{*}|)b(\cos{\theta})\mathbf{1}_{\theta\geq\epsilon}h(v_{*})\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma,

\displaystyle S[h](v):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Phi(|v-v_{*}|)b(\cos{\theta})[h(v_{*})-h(v^{\prime}_{*})]\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma,

\displaystyle S_{\epsilon}[h](v):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Phi(|v-v_{*}|)b(\cos{\theta})\mathbf{1}_{\theta<\epsilon}[h(v_{*})-h(v^{\prime}_{*})]\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma.

With this decomposition of the collision operator, we now introduce the definition of mild solutions in the non-cutoff setting:

Definition 6.1
.

Suppose that
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfies
Assumption A
and the kernel
B
satisfies
Assumption B
with
\nu\geq 0
. Given a non-negative continuous function
f_{0}
on
\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we call a non-negative continuous function
f
defined on
[0,T)\times(\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
with
|f(t,x,v)|\leq C(1+|v|)^{-r}
for some constant
C>0
and
r>3
for any
0<t\leq T
,
(x,v)\in\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
a ”continuous mild” solution to (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) with initial data
f_{0}
if
f
is continuous on
\Gamma_{conti}
and there exists a number
0<\epsilon_{0}<\frac{\pi}{4}
such that for any
0<\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}
,
(t,x,v)\in[0,T)\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we have

(6.6)

\begin{split}f(t,x,v)&=f_{0}(X_{0,t}(x,v),v)\exp\bigg[-\int_{0}^{t}(L_{\epsilon}+S_{\epsilon})[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](v)\,ds\bigg]\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(-\int_{s}^{t}(L_{\epsilon}+S_{\epsilon})[f(s^{\prime},X_{s^{\prime},t}(x,v),\cdot)](v)\,ds^{\prime}\right)\\
&(Q^{+}_{\epsilon}+Q_{\epsilon}^{1})[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot),f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](v)\,ds,\end{split}

when
t\leq t_{\partial}(x,v)
, and

(6.7)

\begin{split}f(t,x,v)&=\alpha f(t-t_{\partial}(x,v),X_{t-t_{\partial}(x,v),t}(x,v),R(X_{t-t_{\partial}(x,v),t}(x,v),v))\\
&\exp\left[-\int_{t-t_{\partial}(x,v)}^{t}(L_{\epsilon}+S_{\epsilon})[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](v)\,ds\right]\\
&+(1-\alpha)\left(\int_{w\cdot n(X_{t-t_{\partial}(x,v),t}(x,v))>0}f(t,X_{t-t_{\partial}(x,v),t}(x,v),w)(w\cdot n(X_{t-t_{\partial}(x,v),t}(x,v)))dw\right)\\
&\frac{1}{2\pi T_{B}^{2}}e^{-\frac{|v|^{2}}{2T_{B}}}\exp\left[-\int_{t-t_{\partial}(x,v)}^{t}(L_{\epsilon}+S_{\epsilon})[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](v)\,ds\right]\\
&+\int_{t-t_{\partial}(x,v)}^{t}\exp\left(-\int_{s}^{t}(L_{\epsilon}+S_{\epsilon})[f(s^{\prime},X_{s^{\prime},t}(x,v),\cdot)](v)\,ds^{\prime}\right)\\
&(Q^{+}_{\epsilon}+Q_{\epsilon}^{2})[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot),f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](v)\,ds\end{split}

when
t\geq t_{\partial}(x,v)
.
Here, we recall the definition of
t_{\partial}(x,v)
from
Definition
1.1
.

Before introducing the main result in the non-cutoff case, we introduce the following constants (here
\widetilde{\gamma}:=(2+\gamma)^{+}
):

(6.8)

e^{\prime}_{f}(t,x):=\int_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v|^{\widetilde{\gamma}}f(t,x,v)\,dv,

(6.9)

E^{\prime}_{f}:=\sup\limits_{[0,T)\times\Omega}e^{\prime}_{f}(t,x),

(6.10)

w_{f}(t,x):=\|f(t,x,\cdot)\|_{W^{2,\infty}_{v}},

(6.11)

W_{f}:=\sup\limits_{[0,T)\times\Omega}w_{f}(t,x).

Now, we introduce our main theorem for the non-cutoff case:

Theorem 6.1
.

Suppose that
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfies
Assumption A
and that the kernel
B
satisfies
Assumption B
with
\nu\geq 0
,
\alpha\in[0,1]
. We consider a non-negative function
f_{0}
that is continuous on
\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Let
f(t,x,v)
be a continuous mild solution to (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) on
[0,T)\times\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, with
T>0
(and the initial condition
f_{0}
) which satisfies the following properties:

(1)

M>0
;

(2)

E_{f}<\infty
, where
p_{\gamma}>\frac{3}{3+\gamma}>0
, if
-3<\gamma<0
;

(3)

W_{f}<\infty
and
E^{\prime}_{f}<\infty
.

Then, the following lower bound holds:
There exists
0<\tau_{0}\leq T
such that for any
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})
and
K>2\frac{\log{(2+\frac{2\nu}{2-\nu})}}{\log{2}}
, there exist
\Delta_{\tau_{0}}>0
,
\rho>0
and
\theta>0
depending on
\tau_{0}
,
C_{\Phi},c_{\Phi},\gamma,b_{0},\nu,E_{f}
,
W_{f}
,
E^{\prime}_{f}
,
M
(and
L_{f,p}
if
\gamma<0
),
\tau
,
K
, and the modulus of continuity of
f_{0}
, such that

(6.12)

f(t,x,v)\geq\frac{\rho}{(2\pi\theta)^{\frac{3}{2}}}e^{-\frac{|v|^{K}}{2\theta}},\ \forall\,t\in[\tau,\Delta_{\tau_{0}}),\ \forall\,(x,v)\in\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}.

In the case when
\nu=0
, we can further take
K=2
.

To prove this theorem, we quote the following result extracted from
Corollary 2.2
in
[
11
]
:

Lemma 6.1
.

Consider
g
a measurable function on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and assume that the collision operator satisfies
Assumption B
with
0\leq\nu<2
. Then, there exists
C_{g}^{S}>0
which depends only on
m_{b}
,
C_{\Phi}
and
e_{g}
(and
l_{g,p}
, where
p>\frac{3}{3+\gamma}
, if
\gamma<0
) such that

(6.13)

|S[g](v)|\leq C_{g}^{S}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}.

Here we recall the definition of
e_{g}
in (
1.16
),
l_{g,p}
in (
1.17
) and define
m_{b}:=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos{\theta})(1-\cos{\theta})\,d\sigma
.

Similarly to the cut-off case, we would like to use (
6.6
) and (
6.7
) to spread the initial lower bounds. However, the additional non-cutoff term
Q^{1}_{\epsilon}
is not necessarily nonnegative. Hence, we need to quote
Lemma 2.5
from
[
11
]
:

Lemma 6.2
.

Suppose that the collision operator satisfies
Assumption B
with
0\leq\nu<2
. Then, there exists a constant
C>0
, which depends only on
\gamma,\ \nu,\ b_{0}
, such that for any measurable function
h_{1},h_{2}
, we have

(6.14)

|Q^{1}_{b}\left(h_{1},h_{2}\right)|\leq Cm_{b}c_{\Phi}\|h_{2}\|_{L^{1}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}}\|h_{1}\|_{W^{2,\infty}}.

Now, we show that a result which is similar to the result of
Proposition
3.2
can be obtained:

Proposition 6.2
.

Suppose that
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfies
Assumption A
and the kernel
B
satisfies
Assumption B
with
0\leq\nu<2
,
\alpha\in[0,1]
. We consider a mild continuous solution
f(t,x,v)
of (
1.1
)–(
1.3
). Given
A>0
,
0<\Delta_{1}\leq 1,

\Delta_{2}<1
, and
(\tau,x^{\prime},v^{\prime})\in[0,T)\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that
B(x^{\prime},\Delta_{2})\subset\Omega
and

f(t,x,v)\geq A,\ \forall(t,x,v)\in[\tau,\tau+\Delta_{1}]\times B(x^{\prime},\Delta_{2})\times B(v^{\prime},\Delta_{2}).

Then, there exist
\{\epsilon_{i}>0\}_{i=0}^{\infty}
such that for
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,
t\in[\tau,\tau+\Delta_{1}]
,
x\in B(x^{\prime},\frac{\Delta_{2}}{2^{n}})
,

(6.15)

\forall v\in\mathbb{R}^{3},\ f(t,x,v)\geq\alpha^{NC}_{n}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},A,|v^{\prime}|)\mathbf{1}_{B(v^{\prime},r_{n}(\Delta_{2}))}(v),

where the numbers
\{r_{n}(\Delta_{2})\}_{n=0}^{\infty}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\{t_{n}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|)\}_{n=0}^{\infty}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\{\alpha^{NC}_{n}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},A,|v^{\prime}|)\}_{n=0}^{\infty}\in\mathbb{R}
are defined as follows:

(6.16)

r_{0}(\Delta_{2}):=\Delta_{2},\ r_{n+1}(\Delta_{2}):=\frac{3\sqrt{2}}{4}r_{n}(\Delta_{2}),

(6.17)

t^{NC}_{n}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|):=\max\left\{\tau,t-\frac{\Delta_{2}}{2^{n+1}(2r_{n}(\Delta_{2})+|v^{\prime}|)}\right\},

(6.18)

\alpha_{0}:=A

(6.19)

\begin{split}&\alpha^{NC}_{n+1}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},A,|v^{\prime}|)\\
\ :=&\frac{1}{4}\int_{t^{NC}_{n}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|)}^{t}\exp\left(-(t-s)(C_{L,1}n_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon_{n}}}+C_{L,2}m_{b^{NCO}_{\epsilon_{n}}})\left\langle|v^{\prime}|+2r_{n}(\Delta_{2})\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}\right)\\
&\hskip 14.22636ptC_{Q,1}\alpha^{NC}_{n}(\tau,s,\Delta_{2},A,|v^{\prime}|)^{2}l_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon_{n}}}c_{\Phi}(r_{n}(\Delta_{2}))^{3+\gamma}\,ds.\end{split}

where
C_{L,1}
,
C_{L,2}
and
C_{Q,1}
are constants which depend on
\gamma,\ \nu,\ b_{0},\ n_{b}
,
m_{b}
,
C_{\Phi}
,
E_{f}
,
W_{f}
,
E^{\prime}_{f}
,
M
(and
L_{f,p}
, where
p>\frac{3}{3+\gamma}
, if
\gamma<0
).

Proof.

We prove the proposition by induction on
n
. The case
n=0
is exactly the assumption. Assume that the statement of
Proposition
6.2
holds for
n=k
. Given
t\in[\tau,\tau+\Delta_{1}]
,
x\in B\left(x^{\prime},\frac{\Delta_{2}}{2^{k+1}}\right)
,
v\in B\left(v^{\prime},r_{k+1}(\Delta_{2})\right)\subset B\left(0,|v^{\prime}|+2r_{k}(\Delta_{2})\right)
, we consider the second term on the right-hand side of (
6.6
) to obtain the following lower bound which holds for
0<\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}
(
\epsilon_{0}
is mentioned in
Definition
6.1
):

(6.20)

\begin{split}&f(t,x,v)\\
=&f(t^{NC}_{k}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|),X_{t^{NC}_{k}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|),t}(x,v),v)\\
&\exp\bigg[-\int_{t^{NC}_{k}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|)}^{t}(L_{\epsilon}+S_{\epsilon})[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](v)\,ds\bigg]\\
&+\int_{t^{NC}_{k}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|)}^{t}\exp\left(-\int_{s}^{t}(L_{\epsilon}+S_{\epsilon})[f(s^{\prime},X_{s^{\prime},t}(x,v),\cdot)](v)\,ds^{\prime}\right)\\
&(Q^{+}_{\epsilon}+Q_{\epsilon}^{1})[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot),f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](v)\,ds,\\
\geq&\int_{t^{NC}_{k}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|)}^{t}\exp\left(-\int_{s}^{t}(L_{\epsilon}+S_{\epsilon})[f(s^{\prime},X_{s^{\prime},t}(x,v),\cdot)](v))\,ds^{\prime}\right)\\
&(Q^{+}_{\epsilon}-|Q_{\epsilon}^{1}|)[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot),f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](v)\,ds.\end{split}

Now, by
Lemma
3.1
and
Lemma
6.1
, we have

(6.21)

|(L_{\epsilon}+S_{\epsilon})[f(s^{\prime},X_{s^{\prime},t}(x,v),\cdot)](v)|\leq(C_{L,1}n_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon}}+C_{L,2}m_{b^{NCO}_{\epsilon}})\left\langle|v^{\prime}|+2r_{k}(\Delta_{2})\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}

for some constants
C_{L,1},C_{L,2}
depending only on
n_{b}
,
m_{b}
,
C_{\Phi}
and
E_{f}
(and
L_{f,p}
if
\gamma<0
).

Hence, the following estimate holds:

(6.22)

\begin{split}&f(t,x,v)\\
\geq&\int_{t^{NC}_{k}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|)}^{t}\exp\left(-(t-s)(C_{L,1}n_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon}}+C_{L,2}m_{b^{NCO}_{\epsilon}})\left\langle|v^{\prime}|+2r_{k}(\Delta_{2})\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}\right)\\
&(Q^{+}_{\epsilon}-|Q_{\epsilon}^{1}|)[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot),f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](v)\,ds,\end{split}

for all
\frac{\pi}{4}>\epsilon>0
.

We notice that when
s\in\left[t^{NC}_{k}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|),t\right]
, we have

|x^{\prime}-X_{s,t}(x,v)|=|x^{\prime}-x+tv-sv|\leq\frac{\Delta_{2}}{2^{k+1}}+|t-s|(|v^{\prime}|+2r_{k}(\Delta_{2}))\leq\frac{\Delta_{2}}{2^{k}},

which implies that
X_{s,t}(x,v)\in B\left(x^{\prime},\frac{\Delta_{2}}{2^{k}}\right)\subset\Omega
and we deduce by induction hypothesis that

(6.23)

\forall v\in\mathbb{R}^{3},\ f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),v)\geq\alpha^{NC}_{k}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},A,|v^{\prime}|)\mathbf{1}_{B(v^{\prime},r_{k}(\Delta_{2}))}(v).

Next, we obtain from
Lemma
3.2
by setting
\xi=\frac{1}{4}
: for any
v\in B\left(v^{\prime},r_{k+1}(\Delta_{2})\right)
, we have

(6.24)

\begin{split}&Q_{\epsilon}^{+}[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot),f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](v)\\
\geq&C_{Q,1}\alpha^{NC}_{k}(\tau,s,\Delta_{2},A,|v^{\prime}|)^{2}l_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon}}c_{\Phi}(r_{k}(\Delta_{2}))^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{B\left(v^{\prime},\frac{3\sqrt{2}}{4}r_{k}(\Delta_{2})\right)}\\
=&C_{Q,1}\alpha^{NC}_{k}(\tau,s,\Delta_{2},A,|v^{\prime}|)^{2}l_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon}}c_{\Phi}(r_{k}(\Delta_{2}))^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{2},\end{split}

for some
C_{Q,1}>0
which only depends on
\gamma,\ \nu,\ b_{0}
.

Now, we use (
6.14
) to derive:

(6.25)

\begin{split}|Q_{\epsilon}^{1}[f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot),f(s,X_{s,t}(x,v),\cdot)](v)|\leq C_{Q,2}m_{b^{NCO}_{\epsilon}}c_{\Phi}E^{\prime}_{f}W_{f},\end{split}

for some
C_{Q,2}>0
, which depends only on
\gamma,\ \nu,\ b_{0}
.

As a result, we deduce that

(6.26)

\begin{split}&f(t,x,v)\\
\geq&\int_{t^{NC}_{k}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|)}^{t}\exp\left(-(t-s)(C_{L,1}n_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon}}+C_{L,2}m_{b^{NCO}_{\epsilon}})\left\langle|v^{\prime}|+2r_{k}(\Delta_{2})\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}\right)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\left(C_{Q,1}\alpha^{NC}_{k}(\tau,s,\Delta_{2},A,|v^{\prime}|)^{2}l_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon}}c_{\Phi}(r_{k}(\Delta_{2}))^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{2}-C_{Q,2}m_{b^{NCO}_{\epsilon}}c_{\Phi}E^{\prime}_{f}W_{f}\right)\,ds\\
\geq&\int_{t^{NC}_{k}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|)}^{t}\exp\left(-(t-s)(C_{L,1}n_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon}}+C_{L,2}m_{b^{NCO}_{\epsilon}})\left\langle|v^{\prime}|+2r_{k}(\Delta_{2})\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}\right)\\
&\hskip 14.22636ptC_{Q,1}\alpha^{NC}_{k}(\tau,s,\Delta_{2},A,|v^{\prime}|)^{2}l_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon}}c_{\Phi}(r_{k}(\Delta_{2}))^{3+\gamma}\frac{1}{2}\,ds\\
-&\int_{t^{NC}_{k}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|)}^{t}\exp\left(-(t-s)(C_{L,1}n_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon}}+C_{L,2}m_{b^{NCO}_{\epsilon}})\left\langle|v^{\prime}|+2r_{k}(\Delta_{2})\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}\right)\\
&\hskip 14.22636ptC_{Q,2}m_{b^{NCO}_{\epsilon}}c_{\Phi}E^{\prime}_{f}W_{f}\,ds,\end{split}

for all
\frac{\pi}{4}>\epsilon>0
.

Then, we notice that when
\frac{\pi}{4}>\epsilon>0
, we ahve

(6.27)

l_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon}}:=\inf\limits_{\frac{1}{4}\pi\leq\theta\leq\frac{3}{4}\pi}b^{CO}_{\epsilon}(\cos{\theta})\geq l_{b},

Thus, we deduce from (
6.4
) and (
6.5
) that

(6.28)

\begin{split}n_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon}}=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b^{CO}_{\epsilon}(\cos{\theta})\,d\sigma\sim\begin{cases}\frac{b_{0}}{\nu}\epsilon^{-\nu},\,\nu\in(0,2),\\
b_{0}|\log{\epsilon}|,\,\nu=0,\end{cases}\end{split}

(6.29)

\begin{split}m_{b^{NCO}_{\epsilon}}=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b^{NCO}_{\epsilon}(\cos{\theta})(1-\cos{\theta})\,d\sigma\sim\begin{cases}\frac{b_{0}}{2-\nu}\epsilon^{2-\nu},\,\nu\in(0,2),\\
\frac{b_{0}}{2}\epsilon^{2},\,\nu=0.\end{cases}\end{split}

Hence, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we can choose
\frac{\pi}{4}>\epsilon_{k}>0
such that

(6.30)

\begin{split}&\int_{t^{NC}_{k}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|)}^{t}\exp\left(-(t-s)(C_{L,1}n_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon_{k}}}+C_{L,2}m_{b^{NCO}_{\epsilon_{k}}})\left\langle|v^{\prime}|+2r_{k}(\Delta_{2})\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}\right)\\
&\hskip 14.22636ptC_{Q,2}m_{b^{NCO}_{\epsilon_{k}}}c_{\Phi}E^{\prime}_{f}W_{f}\,ds\\
\leq&\frac{1}{4}\int_{t^{NC}_{k}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|)}^{t}\exp\left(-(t-s)(C_{L,1}n_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon_{k}}}+C_{L,2}m_{b^{NCO}_{\epsilon_{k}}})\left\langle|v^{\prime}|+2r_{k}(\Delta_{2})\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}\right)\\
&\hskip 14.22636ptC_{Q,1}\alpha^{NC}_{k}(\tau,s,\Delta_{2},A,|v^{\prime}|)^{2}l_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon_{k}}}c_{\Phi}(r_{k}(\Delta_{2}))^{3+\gamma}\,ds\end{split}

and therefore

(6.31)

\begin{split}&f(t,x,v)\\
\geq&\frac{1}{4}\int_{t^{NC}_{k}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},|v^{\prime}|)}^{t}\exp\left(-(t-s)(C_{L,1}n_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon_{k}}}+C_{L,2}m_{b^{NCO}_{\epsilon_{k}}})\left\langle|v^{\prime}|+2r_{k}(\Delta_{2})\right\rangle^{\gamma^{+}}\right)\\
&\hskip 14.22636ptC_{Q,1}\alpha^{NC}_{k}(\tau,s,\Delta_{2},A,|v^{\prime}|)^{2}l_{b^{CO}_{\epsilon_{k}}}c_{\Phi}(r_{k}(\Delta_{2}))^{3+\gamma}\,ds\\
=&\alpha^{NC}_{k+1}(\tau,t,\Delta_{2},A,|v^{\prime}|),\end{split}

for
v\in B(v^{\prime},r_{k+1}(\Delta_{2}))
and we conclude the proof by induction.

∎

With the help of
Proposition
6.2
, we can use the exact same argument from
Proposition
3.3
to
Proposition
3.5
to generate series of lower bounds on
\mathbb{Y}
. Then, we can work as in
Proposition
4.1
to deduce the following.

Proposition 6.3
.

Suppose that
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfies
Assumption A
and that the kernel
B
satisfies
Assumption B
with
\nu\geq 0
. Let
f(t,x,v)
be a continuous mild solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) with
\alpha\in[0,1]
. Fix
\tau\in(0,\Delta^{0})
, with
\Delta^{0}>0
is given in
Proposition
3.1
. Then, there exist
b^{NC}(\tau)
,
\delta_{T}(\tau)>0
, which depend on
\gamma,\ \nu,\ b_{0},\ n_{b}
,
m_{b}

C_{\Phi}
and
E_{f}
,
W_{f}
,
E^{\prime}_{f}
, and
M
(and
L_{f,p}
, where
p>\frac{3}{3+\gamma}
, if
\gamma<0
) such that for
t\in[\tau,\tau+\delta_{T}(\tau)]
,

(6.32)

\forall x\in\partial\Omega,\ \int_{v_{*}\cdot n(x)>0}f(t,x,v_{*})(v_{*}\cdot n(x))\,dv_{*}>b^{NC}(\tau).

Using the same method as in
[
1
,
2
]
(notice that the proof does not require the convexity of
\Omega
), we derive the following.

Lemma 6.3
.

Suppose that
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfies
Assumption A
and the kernel
B
satisfies
Assumption B
with
\nu\geq 0
. Let
f(t,x,v)
be a continuous mild solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) with
\alpha\in[0,1]
. Fix
\tau\in(0,\Delta^{0})
, where
\Delta^{0}>0
is given in
Proposition
3.1
and
\{\xi_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\in(0,1)
. Then, there exist
r^{NC}_{0}>0
,
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
such that
\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\Delta_{i}=1
and that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
t\in[(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i})\tau,\tau]
,
\forall(x,v)\in\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we have

(6.33)

f(t,x,v)\geq a_{n}^{NC}(\tau)\mathbf{1}_{B(0,r^{\prime\prime}_{n})}(v)

Here,

(6.34)

r^{NC}_{n+1}:=\sqrt{2}\,(1-\xi_{n})\,r^{NC}_{n},

(6.35)

a_{n+1}^{NC}(\tau):=\begin{cases}C_{Q}\Delta_{n+1}e^{-\left[C_{L}\left(a^{NC}_{n}(\tau)\right)^{2}\left(r_{n}^{NC}\right)^{3+\gamma-\widetilde{\gamma}}\xi_{n}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]^{\frac{-\nu}{2-\nu}}\left(\sum\limits_{k\geq n+1}\Delta_{k}\right)\left(r_{n+1}^{NC}\right)^{\gamma^{+}}}\\
\times\left(a^{NC}_{n}(\tau)\right)^{2}\left(r_{n}^{NC}\right)^{\gamma+3}\xi_{n}^{\frac{5}{2}},&\nu\in(0,2),\\
C_{Q}\Delta_{n+1}e^{-C\log\left[C_{L}\left(a^{NC}_{n}(\tau)\right)^{2}\left(r_{n}^{NC}\right)^{3+\gamma-\widetilde{\gamma}}\xi_{n}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\left(\sum\limits_{k\geq n+1}\Delta_{k}\right)\left(r_{n+1}^{NC}\right)^{\gamma^{+}}}\\
\times\left(a^{NC}_{n}(\tau)\right)^{2}\left(r_{n}^{NC}\right)^{\gamma+3}\xi_{n}^{\frac{5}{2}},&\nu=0.\end{cases}

Finally, by proceeding as in
[
11
]
, p.29–31, we can derive the ”weaker than Maxwellian” lower bound of
Theorem
6.1
.

A.
Appendix: the characteristic line

In this section, we recall some useful properties about the bounce trajectory of
\Omega
used in the definition of the characteristic line
X_{s,t}(x,v)
and
V_{s,t}(x,v)
.
To begin with, we introduce some notations from Definition A.1 in
[
2
]
.

Definition A.1
.

Given an open bounded domain with a
C^{1}
boundary, we define a partition of
\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
as follows:

•

\Omega_{rebounds}:=\{(x,v)\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\mid v\cdot n(x)<0\}.

•

\Omega_{rolling}:=\{(x,v)\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\mid v\cdot n(x)=0,\ \exists\,\delta>0\ s.t.\ x-vt\in\overline{\Omega},\ \forall\,t\in[0,\delta]\}.

•

\Omega_{stop}:=\{(x,v)\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\mid v\cdot n(x)=0,\ \forall\,\delta>0\ \exists\,t\in(0,\delta)\ s.t.\ x-vt\notin\overline{\Omega}\}.

•

\Omega_{line}:=\{(x,v)\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\mid v\cdot n(x)>0\}.

Due to
Proposition A.2
in
[
2
]
, there is no specular reflection trajectory that leads to any point in
\Omega_{stop}
.
Given
(x,v)\in\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we recall the definition of
t_{\partial}(x,v)

t_{\partial}(x,v)=\max\{t\geq 0\mid x-vs\in\overline{\Omega},\forall\,s\in[0,t]\}.

The next proposition(
Proposition A.3
in
[
2
]
) gives a picture of a backward trajectory touching the boundary:

Proposition A.1
.

Given an open bounded domain in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
with
C^{1}
boundary. Then, we have

(1) If there exists
t\in(0,t_{\partial}(x,v))
such that
x-vt\in\partial\Omega
, then
(x-vt,v)\in\Omega_{rolling}
.

(2)
t_{\partial}(x,v)=0
if and only if
(x,v)\in\Omega_{stop}\cup\Omega_{rebound}
.

(3)
(x-vt_{\partial},v)\in\Omega_{stop}\cup\Omega_{rebound}
.

We always need to consider some strange trajectory with a strange path.
Thankfully, the following proposition (
Proposition A.4 in
[
2
]
) shows that we do not need to worry about the case that the backward trajectory reaching
\Omega_{stop}
or bounce infinite times in a finite time since those set are measure zero:

Proposition A.2
.

We consider an open bounded domain in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
with
C^{1}
boundary and
(x,v)\in\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Then, for any
t\geq 0
the trajectory of
(x,v)
with specular reflection after time
t
has at most a countable number of rebound and rolling. Moreover, the set with infinite rebound and rolling in finite time is zero measure in
\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Remark A.1
.

Similarly, with the above assumption the subset of
\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
with a backward trajectory leading to
\Omega_{stop}
is also measure zero in
\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Now, we introduce the characteristic line by defining:

(A.1)

(t_{0},x_{0},v_{0}):=(0,x,v),

(A.2)

\begin{split}&(t_{k+1},x_{k+1},v_{k+1})\\
:=&\begin{cases}(\infty,x_{k},v_{k}),\ &(x_{k},v_{k})\in\Omega_{stop},\\
(t_{k}+t_{\partial}(x_{k},v_{k}),x_{k}-v_{k}t_{\partial}(x_{k},v_{k}),R(x_{k}-v_{k}t_{\partial}(x_{k},v_{k}),v_{k})),\ &(x_{k},v_{k})\notin\Omega_{stop},\end{cases}\end{split}

(A.3)

n(t,x,v):=\sup\{k\in\mathbb{N}:\ t_{k}(x,v)\leq t\}.

Note that
n(t,x,v)<\infty
in case
\{t_{k}\}
is unbounded.

Next, we define the last rebound from the backward trajectory of
(x,v)
:

(A.4)

(t_{fin},x_{fin},v_{fin}):=\begin{cases}(t,x_{n},v_{n}),&n(t,x,v)<\infty,\ \,t_{n(t,x,v)+1}=\infty,\\
(t_{n},x_{n},v_{n}),&n(t,x,v)<\infty,\ \,t_{n(t,x,v)+1}<\infty,\\
\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}(t_{n},x_{n},v_{n}),&n(t,x,v)=\infty.\end{cases}

Finally, for
0\leq s\leq t
, we define the characteristic line :

\displaystyle\begin{cases}X_{t}(x,v)&:=x_{fin}(t,x,-v)-(t-t_{fin}(t,x,-v))v_{fin}(t,x,-v),\\
V_{t}(x,v)&:=-v_{fin}(t,x,-v).\end{cases}

\displaystyle\begin{cases}X_{s,t}(x,v)&:=X_{s}(X_{t}(x,-v),-V_{t}(x,-v)),\\
V_{s,t}(x,v)&:=V_{s}(X_{t}(x,-v),-V_{t}(x,-v)).\end{cases}

Data availability statement

Data sharing is not applicable to this article, as no data sets were generated or analyzed during the current study.
